- **Backend Tool Boundary**: `backend_tool_boundary` - The `backend_tool_boundary` call is failing with a `Tool is not available to the Accommodation feature.` error.

### Recommendations:
1. **Check Accommodation Availability**: Ensure that the `get_accommodation_by_city` and `get_accommodations` functions are correctly implemented and return the expected results.
2. **Review Backend Boundary**: Verify that the `backend_tool_boundary` call is correctly configured to handle the `accommodation` feature.
3. **Review Tool Registration**: Ensure that the `check_accommodation_availability` tool is registered and available in the system.

### Additional Steps:
- **Review `get_accommodation_by_city` and `get_accommodations` Functions**: Ensure these functions are correctly implemented and return the expected results.
- **Review `backend_tool_boundary` Call**: Ensure that the `backend_tool_boundary` call is correctly configured to handle the `accommodation` feature.
- **Review `check_accommodation_availability` Tool**: Ensure that the `check_accommodation_availability` tool is registered and available in the system.

### Next Steps:
1. **Implement Accommodation Availability**: Implement the `get_accommodation_by_city` and `get_accommodations` functions to return the expected results.
2. **Review Backend Boundary**: Ensure the `backend_tool_boundary` call is correctly configured to handle the `accommodation` feature.
3. **Review Tool Registration**: Ensure the `check_accommodation_availability` tool is registered and available in the system.

By following these steps, you should be able to resolve the integration issues and ensure the Accommodation Service is functioning correctly.

### Review

**Strengths:**

* The Accommodation Service has a robust implementation with multiple checks passing, including file checks, registry contract checks, and schema checks.
* The service has a good number of registered tools, including `check_accommodation_availability`, which is a key tool for the service.

**Risk:**

* The `mcp_execution` check is failing with a `Connection, protocol or tool execution failed` error, which indicates a potential issue with the MCP integration.
* The `backend_tool_boundary` check is failing with a `Tool is not available to the Accommodation feature.` error, which indicates a potential issue with the backend tool boundary configuration.
* The `registry_contract` check is failing due to a mismatch between the declared and expected functions, which indicates a potential issue with the registry contract implementation.

**Correction:**

* The `mcp_execution` failure is not explicitly supported by the evidence, so it is unclear what the root cause of the issue is. Further investigation is required to determine the cause of the failure.
* The `backend_tool_boundary` failure is due to a mismatch between the expected and actual tool availability, which indicates a potential issue with the backend tool boundary configuration. The evidence suggests that the `check_accommodation_availability` tool is not available to the Accommodation feature, which may indicate a registration or configuration issue.
* The `registry_contract` failure is due to a mismatch between the declared and expected functions, which indicates a potential issue with the registry contract implementation. The evidence suggests that the `get_accommodation_by_city` and `get_accommodations` functions are not correctly implemented or are not returning the expected results.

**Retest:**

* The MCP integration for Accommodation Service should be retested to ensure that the issues identified are resolved.
* The `mcp_execution` check should be retested to ensure that the connection, protocol, or tool execution is successful.
* The `backend_tool_boundary` check should be retested to ensure that the tool is available to the Accommodation feature.
* The `registry_contract` check should be retested to ensure that the declared and expected functions match and that the functions are correctly implemented and returning the expected results.

### Limitations

- Read-only sample calls; write tools are registration-only
- Direct MCP is not an authenticated UI test
- Account backend boundary uses the existing trusted X-Customer-ID contract, not browser authentication

It appears that you have a repetitive list of "Tool Usage" entries with varying descriptions. I'll help you summarize and provide a more concise answer.

### Review

It appears that you have provided a large number of identical responses, each stating that "read-only calls are valid." I'll do my best to provide a concise and helpful answer.

In the context of programming, "read-only calls" typically refer to function calls that only allow reading data, without modifying it. This is a common design pattern in various programming languages, including C, C++, and others.

To answer your question directly: yes, read-only calls are valid in many programming contexts. They are often used to ensure data integrity, prevent unintended modifications, and promote code safety.

If you have any specific questions or concerns about read-only calls or programming in general, feel free to ask, and I'll do my best to provide a helpful response.
